1-10 月财政数据点评

10 月当月政府性基金支出同比增速加快   
10 月公共预算收入同比增速加快。个人所得税对 10 月当月税收贡献明显。
公共财政仍有待向“稳增长”倾斜。10 月新增财政存款 1.4 万亿元，规模较 9
月明显抬升，有望对 11-12 月政府性基金支出形成支撑。 
 10 月公共财政收入 23379.0 亿元，同比增长 3.4%，增速较 9 月转正加快
3.5 个百分点；其中当月税收收入 21916.0 亿元，同比增长 5.9%，增速较
9 月明显加快 5.7 个百分点；非税收入 1463.0 亿元，同比下滑 23.6%，降
幅较 9 月走扩 22.5 个百分点。 
 10 月全国一般公共预算支出 17843.0 亿元，同比增长 0.5%，增速较 9 月
加快 0.4 个百分点；其中，地方公共财政支出 14055.0 亿元，同比增长 0.1%，
增速较 9 月转正加速 0.9 个百分点。 
“稳增长”方面，当月公共财政支出投向基建领域的占比为 18.2%，较 9
月回落 1.7 个百分点；具体来看，10 月公共财政投向农林水事务的支出
同比增长 6.4%，增速较上月加速 6.6 个百分点，是唯一正增长的基建相
关支出分项；当期公共财政投向城乡社区事务及交通运输领域的支出同
比增速均为负，公共财政仍有待向基建领域倾斜。 
 10 月中央政府性基金收入 875.0 亿元，同比增长 155.8 %，增速较 9 月明
显改善 142.1 个百分点；地方政府性基金收入 3238.0 亿元，同比下滑
5.2%，降幅较 9 月收窄 13.3 个百分点；国有土地使用权出让收入 2439.0
亿元，同比下降 9.0%，降幅收窄 14.2 个百分点。 
 10 月政府性基金支出 6992.0 亿元，同比增长 17.2%，增幅较 9 月明显改
善 29.5 个百分点；中央政府性基金支出 537.0 亿元，同比增长 13.3%，增
速较 9 月加快 5.8 个百分点；地方政府性基金预算支出 6455.0 亿元，同
比增长 17.5 %，增速较 9 月明显加快 30.6 个百分点；国有土地使用权出
让收入相关支出 2354.0 亿元，同比下降 0.6%，降幅较 9 月收窄 18.1 个百
分点。 
 10 月政府性基金支出同比增速加快。1-10 月广义财政支出累计同比降幅
为 2.9%，降幅较 9 月收窄 0.6 个百分点，但仍相对偏弱。中央财政支出
在 1-10 月广义财政支出中占比 13.7%，仍是重要支撑。1-10 月广义中央
财政支出同比下滑 5.7%，降幅较 9 月有所收窄，但对当期广义财政支出
同比增速仍有拖累。 
从不同预算看，首先，1-10 月一般公共预算支出在全年公共预算支出中
占比 76.0%，支出力度整体不弱，持平于去年同期；但 10 月当月支出在
全年公共预算支出中占比为 5.9%，仍低于 2024-2025 年同期水平，10 月
公共财政支出节奏仍较克制。其次，10 月政府性基金支出同比增长
17.2%，增速明显加快。 
融资方面，10 月政府债务融资节奏加快，当月社融口径政府债融资规模
1.1 万亿元，较去年同期增加 6983.0 亿元；值得一提的是，10 月新增财
政存款 1.4 万亿元，规模较 9 月明显抬升，有望对 11-12 月政府性基金支
出形成支撑。 
 风险提示：海外大宗商品价格震荡；地缘关系的不确定性；政策效果不
及预期。

资料来源：wind，中银证券

10 月公共预算收入同比增速加快 
财政部发布数据，2026 年 1-10 月，全国一般公共预算收入 195671.0 亿元，同比增长 4.9%，增速（较
前三季度，下同）放缓 0.2 个百分点。其中，中央一般公共预算收入 88036.0 亿元，同比增长 7.5%，
增速放缓 0.8 个百分点；地方一般公共预算本级收入 107635.0 亿元，同比增长 2.9%，增速小幅加快
0.2 个百分点。 
单月数据方面，10 月公共财政收入 23379.0 亿元，同比增长 3.4%，增速较 9 月转正加快 3.5 个百分
点；其中当月税收收入 21916.0 亿元，同比增长 5.9%，增速较 9 月明显加快 5.7 个百分点；非税收
入 1463.0 亿元，同比下滑 23.6%，降幅较 9 月走扩 22.5 个百分点。

图表 2.财政收入累计同比增速表现  图表 3. 税收、非税收入当月同比增速表现

资料来源：wind，中银证券  资料来源：wind，中银证券

个人所得税对 10 月当月税收贡献明显。首先，国内增值税仍是占比第一大的税收品种。10 月当月，
国内增值税同比下滑 0.1%，增速较 9 月有所下滑，我们认为，增值税同比增速短期或受企业采购、
税款入库时点错位等因素影响，单月波动或属正常现象。值得注意的是，10 月 PPI、工业增加值当
月同比分别增长 5.7%和 4.8%，有望支撑当月工业企业产值，后续国内增值税同比增速有望回升。 
其次，10 月进口环节增值税和消费税同比增长 17.8%，拉动当月税收同比增速 2.4 个百分点；据海
关总署数据显示，10 月我国进口同比增速为 41.6%，对进口环节增值税收入形成支撑。 
此外，10 月国内消费税收入同比增长 4.1%，拉动当月税收同比增速 0.4 百分点；值得说明的是，消费税
收入尚不具备持续修复的基础， 10 月社零同比增速仅为 0.4%，较 9 月放缓，国内需求仍较弱势。 
其他权重税种方面，10 月个人所得税收入同比增长 89.5%，对当月税收同比增速的正贡献为 10.4 个
百分点，贡献明显，10 月个人所得税增量主要来自离岸信托个人所得税补缴。

2026 年 11 月 21 日 1-10 月财政数据点评 3

图表 4. 权重税种收入累计同比增速表现  图表 5.地产相关税种累计同比增速表现

资料来源：wind，中银证券  资料来源：wind，中银证券

公共财政仍有待向“稳增长”倾斜 
2026 年 1-10 月，全国一般公共预算支出 228042.0 亿元，同比增长 1.0%，增幅（较前三季度，下同）
持平；其中，中央一般公共预算本级支出 36608.0 亿元，同比增长 5.4%，增幅收窄 0.4 个百分点；
地方一般公共预算支出 191434.0 亿元，同比增长 0.2%，增幅持平。

图表 6.财政支出累计同比增速表现  图表 7. 2026 年 1-10 月财政支出同比增速结构变化

资料来源：wind，中银证券  资料来源：wind，中银证券

单月数据方面，10 月全国一般公共预算支出 17843.0 亿元，同比增长 0.5%，增速较 9 月加快 0.4
个百分点；其中，地方公共财政支出 14055.0 亿元，同比增长 0.1%，增速较 9 月转正加速 0.9 个
百分点。 
“稳增长”方面，当月公共财政支出投向基建领域的占比为 18.2%，较 9 月回落 1.7 个百分点；具体
来看，10 月公共财政投向农林水事务的支出同比增长 6.4%，增速较上月加速 6.6 个百分点，是唯一
正增长的基建相关支出分项；当期公共财政投向城乡社区事务及交通运输领域的支出同比增速均为
负，公共财政仍有待向基建领域倾斜。 
此外，教育、社保就业支出维持较强刚性，均仍为 10 月公共财政支出的重点方向，占比分别为 14.8%
和 15.8%。

### Page 4

2026 年 11 月 21 日 1-10 月财政数据点评 4

图表 8. 社保就业相关支出累计同比增速表现  图表 9.基建领域相关支出累计同比增速表现

资料来源：wind，中银证券  资料来源：wind，中银证券

土地收入对 1-10 月地方政府性基金收入仍有拖累 
2026 年 1-10 月，全国政府性基金预算收入 29123.0 亿元，同比下降 15.5%，降幅（较前三季度，下
同）收窄 3.1 个百分点。分中央和地方看，中央政府性基金预算收入 4557.0 亿元，同比增长 25.9%，
增幅加速 13.5 个百分点；地方政府性基金预算本级收入 24566.0 亿元，同比下滑 20.4%，降幅收窄
1.9 个百分点，其中，国有土地使用权出让收入 18526.0 亿元，同比下降 25.8%，降幅收窄 2.1 个百
分点，但需注意的是，土地收入对 1-10 月地方政府性基金收入仍有较明显拖累，负贡献为 20.9 个百
分点。 
单月数据方面，10 月中央政府性基金收入 875.0 亿元，同比增长 155.8 %，增速较 9 月明显改善 142.1
个百分点；地方政府性基金收入 3238.0 亿元，同比下滑 5.2%，降幅较 9 月收窄 13.3 个百分点；国
有土地使用权出让收入 2439.0 亿元，同比下降 9.0%，降幅收窄 14.2 个百分点。

图表 10. 政府性基金预算收入当月规模表现  图表 11. 地方政府性基金预算收入当月同比增速表现

资料来源：wind，中银证券  资料来源：wind，中银证券

支出方面，2026 年 1-10 月，全国政府性基金预算支出 69746.0 亿元，同比下滑 13.8%，降幅（相较
于前三季度，下同）收窄 2.4 个百分点；中央本级政府性基金支出 4229.0 亿元，同比下降 50.6%，
降幅较上月收窄 3.8 个百分点，中央财政支出力度相对温和；地方政府性基金预算支出 65517.0 亿元，
同比下降 9.4%，降幅收窄 2.2 个百分点，国有土地使用权出让收入相关支出 27919.0 亿元，同比下
降 17.3%，降幅收窄 1.2 个百分点。

### Page 5

2026 年 11 月 21 日 1-10 月财政数据点评 5

单月数据方面，10 月政府性基金支出 6992.0 亿元，同比增长 17.2%，增幅较 9 月明显改善 29.5 个百
分点；中央政府性基金支出 537.0 亿元，同比增长 13.3%，增速较 9 月加快 5.8 个百分点；地方政府
性基金预算支出 6455.0 亿元，同比增长 17.5 %，增速较 9 月明显加快 30.6 个百分点；国有土地使用
权出让收入相关支出 2354.0 亿元，同比下降 0.6%，降幅较 9 月收窄 18.1 个百分点。

图表 12. 政府性基金预算支出当月规模表现  图表 13. 政府性基金预算支出当月同比增速表现

资料来源：wind，中银证券  资料来源：wind，中银证券

10 月政府性基金支出同比增速加快。1-10 月广义财政支出累计同比降幅为 2.9%，降幅较 9 月收窄
0.6 个百分点，但仍相对偏弱。中央财政支出在 1-10 月广义财政支出中占比 13.7%，仍是重要支撑。
1-10 月广义中央财政支出同比下滑 5.7%，降幅较 9 月有所收窄，但对当期广义财政支出同比增速仍
有拖累。 
从不同预算看，首先，1-10 月一般公共预算支出在全年公共预算支出中占比 76.0%，支出力度整体
不弱，持平于去年同期；但 10 月当月支出在全年公共预算支出中占比为 5.9%，仍低于 2024-2025
年同期水平，10 月公共财政支出节奏仍较克制。其次，10 月政府性基金支出同比增长 17.2%，增速
明显加快。 
融资方面，10 月政府债务融资节奏加快，当月社融口径政府债融资规模 1.1 万亿元，较去年同期增
加 6983.0 亿元；值得一提的是，10 月新增财政存款 1.4 万亿元，规模较 9 月明显抬升，有望对 11-12
月政府性基金支出形成支撑。

评级体系说明 
以报告发布日后公司股价/行业指数涨跌幅相对同期相关市场指数的涨跌幅的表现为基准： 
公司投资评级： 
买    入：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 20%以上； 
增    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 10%-20%； 
中    性：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数变动幅度在-10%-10%之间； 
减    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数跌幅在 10%以上； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

行业投资评级： 
强于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现强于基准指数； 
中    性：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现基本与基准指数持平； 
弱于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现弱于基准指数； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

壓抑，AMD 資料中心營收近倍增、高通財報後走弱。美股賣壓延燒，台股連日重挫、新台幣

張，惟價格分項仍處高檔；台灣 10 月製造業 PMI 連續 13 個月擴張但速度趨緩，CPI 年增率
降至 2.33%。看法：美國景氣與通膨黏性支撐 Fed 偏鷹立場，台灣央行壓力相對減輕；後續

間，美國公債仍具投資組合避險功能，但考量通膨與能源價格的不確定性，現階段不建議延
長存續期間。中天期美國公債及高信評投資等級債在兼顧票息收益、利率敏感度與資產配置

仍較為穩健，對 AI 相關企業則須進一步評估資本支出、合約現金流與債務到期結構。

Indicators Update 
2026年 7-9月期 GDP（1次速報） 
個人消費の弱さと外需の下押しで、4四半期ぶりのマイナス成長に

経済調査部 チーフエコノミスト 神田 慶司 
  エコノミスト 畑中 宏仁

[要約] 
◼ 2026年 7-9月期の実質 GDP成長率は前期比年率▲0.1％（前期比▲0.0％）と小幅なが
らも 4 四半期ぶりのマイナス成長となった。設備投資が増加した一方、個人消費が 2
四半期連続で減少し、輸出減と輸入増から外需も成長率を押し下げた。在庫変動を除
く実質最終需要は前期比年率▲2.1％と低迷するなど、内容は悪い。 
◼ 10-12月期の実質 GDP成長率は前期比年率＋0％台前半と小幅ながらもプラス成長に転
じると見込んでいる。個人消費は小幅に増加し、設備投資も増加するだろう。輸出は
米国向けを中心に増加するものの、輸入の伸びが上回り、外需の寄与度は 2四半期連
続のマイナスになるとみている。リスク要因は AI関連需要の下振れと中東情勢の悪化
であり、輸出や個人消費などへの影響に注意が必要だ。

※当社は、11月 20日（金）に「第231回日本経済予測」の発表を予定している。

実質GDPは小幅ながらも 4四半期ぶりのマイナス成長 
実質GDP成長率は前期比年率▲0.1％と市場予想を下回り、内容も悪い 
2026年 7-9月期の実質 GDP成長率は、1次速報値で前期比年率▲0.1％（前期比▲0.0％）だ
った。小幅ながらも 4四半期ぶりのマイナス成長となり、市場予想（QUICK調査中央値、前期
比年率＋0.2％）を下回った。なお、12月 8日に公表される 2次速報では 2025年度国民経済計
算年次推計の反映により、幅広い需要項目が改定される可能性がある。 
設備投資が増加に転じ、実質雇用者報酬の増加が続くなど緩やかな景気の拡大基調は続いて
いるとみられるが、在庫変動を除いた実質最終需要は前期比年率▲2.1％であり、個人消費が 2
四半期連続で減少するなど内容は悪い。 
また、交易利得・損失は7-9月期で▲11.4兆円（年率換算額）で1、実質GDPに交易利得・損
失を加えた実質 GDI（国内総所得）成長率は前期比年率▲1.6％だった。原油高などによる交易
条件の悪化で所得が海外に流出し、交易損失は前期から 2.2兆円拡大した。国内の所得環境は
GDPで見る以上に悪化したが、政府のエネルギー高対策が家計や企業への影響を一定程度緩和
した。 
実質 GDPを需要項目別に見ると（図表 2）、民需関連では個人消費・住宅投資が減少した一方、
設備投資は増加した。公需関連では政府消費と公共投資が減少した。公的在庫変動については、
7-9月期に大規模な石油の国家備蓄放出が行われなかったため、実質 GDP成長率への寄与度は
前期比＋0.5％pt と成長率を大きく押し上げた。外需関連では輸出減と輸入増により、純輸出
は実質GDP成長率を押し下げた。 
図表2：実質 GDP成長率と需要項目別の寄与度

（出所）内閣府統計より大和総研作成

1 交易条件（＝輸出デフレーター/輸入デフレーター）の変化による実質所得の増減で、プラスの場合は「交易 
利得」、マイナスの場合は「交易損失」。公表値は 2020暦年連鎖価格のデフレーターから算出されているた 
め（基準年の交易利得・損失額はゼロ）、交易利得・損失額はあくまでも基準年からの交易条件の変化の影響 
である点に留意が必要。

### Page 3

1

3 / 7

GDPデフレーターは前年同期比＋2.4％と 19四半期連続のプラスだった。また、単位労働コ
スト（＝名目雇用者報酬÷実質 GDP）は同＋4.4％と 14四半期連続のプラスで、賃金面からの
物価上昇圧力は足元でも継続している（巻末の関連指標①下段左）。

個人消費は、4-6月期が下方修正された上で 2四半期連続の減少 
個人消費は前期比▲0.3％と 2四半期連続で減少した。公表前の 4-6月期の個人消費は同＋
0.0％だったが、インバウンド消費額が上方修正されたことで同▲0.3％へと引き下げられた2。
7-9月期は一部耐久財の反動減や、9 月の天候不順といった一時的な下押し要因があったもの
の、所得環境が改善する中で 4-6月期から 2四半期連続で減少したところに、個人消費の弱さ
が見られる。 
国内家計最終消費支出を財・サービス別に見ると、耐久財（前期比▲3.5％）や半耐久財
（同▲1.4％）、非耐久財（同▲0.2％）がいずれも減少した一方、サービス（同＋0.1％）は増
加した（巻末の関連指標①上段左）。内閣府によると、耐久財ではエアコンなどが、半耐久財
では衣服などが、非耐久財ではその他個人ケア用器具（歯ブラシなど）などが押し下げたとい
う。4-6月期には、自動車税の環境性能割廃止や、2027年 4月に予定されているエアコンの省
エネ基準引き上げを見越した買い替え需要が耐久財消費を後押ししたが、その効果がはく落し
た。衣料品などの不振は天候不順が影響したとみられる。一方、サービスでは教育や携帯電話
通信料などが押し上げたという。 
実質雇用者報酬（家計最終消費支出デフレーターで実質化）は前期比＋0.3％と6四半期連続
で増加した（巻末の関連指標①上段右）。労働力調査（総務省）に見る雇用者数は同 0.0％で、
雇用者数で除した1人あたり実質雇用者報酬は同＋0.3％と6四半期連続で増加した（前年同期
比では3四半期連続のプラス）。

設備投資は3四半期ぶりの増加、住宅投資は2四半期連続の減少 
設備投資は前期比＋1.6％と 3四半期ぶりに増加した。建設投資は減少したものの、4-6月期
に特殊要因で減少していた研究開発投資で反動増が表れたほか、機械投資が増加したようだ。
内閣府によると、機械投資はサーバーや半導体製造装置などが増加に寄与したという。 
住宅投資は前期比▲1.1％と2四半期連続で減少した。原材料費高騰による建設資材価格の上
昇や人件費の上昇が重しとなっており、住宅着工戸数は 6月にかけて増加していたものの、7
月以降は下落に転じている（巻末の関連指標②上段右）。

2 家計最終消費支出は国内消費支出から非居住者家計の国内での直接購入（インバウンド消費）を控除して算
出されているため、インバウンド消費の上方修正は家計最終消費支出の下方修正につながる。

### Page 4

1

4 / 7

公共投資は 2四半期連続の減少、政府消費は6四半期ぶりに減少 
公共投資は前期比▲1.1％と2四半期連続で減少した。資材価格の高騰や人手不足などが重し
になっている上、2025年度補正予算に盛り込まれた公共事業の執行が一巡したとみられる。 
政府消費は前期比▲0.0％と小幅ながらも6四半期ぶりに減少した。内閣府によると、医療・
介護費が増加したものの、中間消費を中心にデフレーターが上昇したことが実質成長率の押し
下げにつながったという。 
また、実質 GDP成長率に対する公的在庫変動の寄与度は、7-9月期は大規模な石油の国家備
蓄放出が行われなかったため前期比＋0.5％ptとなり、成長率を大幅に押し上げた。後述のよ
うに原油の代替調達の進展によって、輸入の増加が実質 GDP成長率を押し下げたが、公的在庫
変動の増加がおおむね相殺した形だ。

輸出減と輸入増により、外需寄与度は前期比▲0.6％pt 
輸出は前期比▲0.2％と4四半期ぶりに減少した。財は増加したが、サービスが減少した。内
閣府によると、財では半導体製造装置や石油製品、基礎化学製品などが増加したという。サー
ビスでは、4-6月期に特殊要因で大幅に増加していた研究開発サービスに加え、インバウンド
や産業財産権等使用料なども減少したようだ。 
輸入は前期比＋2.5％と 2 四半期ぶりに増加した。サービスは減少したものの、財が増加し
た。財では、中東情勢の影響により 4-6月期に減少していた石油・天然ガスが代替調達の進展
によって増加したほか、非鉄金属やサーバー等も増加したようだ。サービスでは、アウトバウ
ンドは増加したものの、内閣府によると著作権等使用料や研究開発サービス、専門・経営コン
サルティングサービスなどが減少したという。 
輸出の減少と輸入の増加により、純輸出（外需）の実質 GDP成長率に対する寄与度は前期比
▲0.6％ptと、4四半期ぶりのマイナスとなった。

10-12月期の実質 GDPは内需と輸出に支えられ、小幅のプラス成長か 
2026年 10-12月期の実質 GDP成長率は前期比年率＋0％台前半と、小幅ながらもプラス成長
に転じると見込んでいる。 
個人消費は小幅な増加に転じるとみている。現金給与総額は前年同月比で 3％を超える伸び
が続いており、10月以降は各地で最低賃金が引き上げられていることから名目賃金の高い伸び
は続くだろう。一方、食料品等を中心に物価上昇率は高まっている。エネルギー価格について
も、資源価格高騰がラグを持って反映されることに加え、補助の一時終了や縮小も物価の押し
上げ要因となり、10-12月期の実質賃金上昇率を押し下げるだろう。設備投資は建設資材価格
と人件費の上昇が建設投資を下押しするものの、省力化投資やAI関連投資を背景に増加基調が

### Page 5

1

5 / 7

続くと見込む。 
輸出は増加に転じるとみている。米国の設備投資はAI関連需要を背景に増加が続くだろう。
米国内での設備投資増加は米国向け輸出の追い風となり、財輸出は増加すると見込んでいる。
一方、インバウンドは中国政府による日本への渡航自粛要請が続く中、増加は緩やかなものと
なるだろう。 
輸入は増加するとみている。原油輸入の回復が続くことに加え、AI関連需要を背景に電算機
類等の輸入も増加するだろう。輸入の増加が輸出の増加を上回ることで、外需の寄与度は 2四
半期連続のマイナスになると見込んでいる。 
10-12月期以降を含めたリスク要因は、AI関連需要の下振れと中東情勢の悪化である。足元
の米国経済では設備投資が景気を下支えしており、とりわけデータセンターや半導体などの IT
関連投資がけん引役となっている。AI関連サービスの世界的な利用拡大に伴い、計算能力の増
強に向けた大型投資が今後も続く可能性は高い。一方で、一部のAI関連企業では収益性や財務
面への懸念が広がっているほか、米中間選挙でAIデータセンター建設の一時停止や規制強化な
どを訴えた民主党急進左派候補者が当選するなど、AI投資を取り巻く環境には変化の兆しも見
られる。ハイパースケーラーが設備投資の拡大ペースを落とせば、半導体や情報通信機器など
の需要を下押しし、関連産業の生産減や輸出減などを通じて実体経済にも波及するだろう3。ま
た、中東情勢が悪化し、原油価格がさらに高騰すれば、物価高を通じて個人消費を一段と下押
しするだろう。 
2027年度までの経済見通しの詳細などについては、11月 20日に発表予定の「第 231回日本
経済予測」を参照されたい。

3 詳細は、畑中宏仁・神田慶司「ハイパースケーラーの投資動向が世界経済に与える影響」（大和総研レポー
ト、2026年11月 12日）を参照。

Key Data  _____________________________________

GS Forecast  __________________________________

GS Factor Profile  ______________________________

Lotes released its 3Q26 results on Nov 12th and held a results 
call on Nov 13th. Key takeaways from management include: 
(1) Lotes expects GM to begin recovering starting from 4Q26 along 
with the ramp up of AMD Venice CPU, with 4Q26 GM expected to 
reach similar levels as 2Q26 (GM was 44.8% in 3Q26, down from 
45.5% in 2Q26), and expects 4Q26 revenue to see low single digit 
QoQ growth. 
(2) Management is positive on the outlook for its server business in 
2027, expecting YoY growth to accelerate compared with 2026 
(server business with 57% YoY in 2026E per GSe), and expects 
server business to account for 60%+ of total 2027 revenue (vs. 54% 
in 2026E per GSe). The solid growth will be driven by (1) server unit 
growth of 10-15% in 2027 (up from ~10% in 2026), (2) ASP increase 
across new server CPU products (including server CPU sockets, PCIe 
cables and connectors), and (3) rising contribution from new 
AI-related products (including QD, SOCAMM and new NPO 
solutions). 
(3) The company is conservative on its 2027 GM outlook, indicating 
that overall GM is unlikely to reach the 50%+ level, mainly due to a 
higher contribution from margin-dilutive products (QD and 
SOCAMM) and delay in the ramp up of next gen server CPU products 
(MP timing for Intel Oak Stream has been delayed and will begin by 
late 1Q27 or early 2Q27). 
(4) Despite QD being margin dilutive (QD with 35-40% GM per GSe), 
management is positive that this product will drive revenue/EPS 
growth going forward. Management indicated that QD contributed 
~2% of 3Q26 revenue (with 150k+ pieces of monthly shipment per 
GSe vs. ~15k pieces of shipment in 2025). Management expects 
revenue contribution from QD will increase to 3-5% by December 
and further expand to ~10% in 2027. UTR for QD is currently full, 
and the company plans to double the capacity over the coming

### Page 3

quarters.
(5) The company expects NPO products to enter MP in 2027, although their revenue
contribution is likely to be <2%. Management anticipates revenue contribution to
increase by multiple folds in 2028, driven by broader adoption of 6.4T NPO products,
which are expected to have higher ASP than the mainstream 3.2T NPO products in 2027.
(6) For the CPU platform, Lotes mentioned Eagle Stream’s penetration is currently at
~50% (was 40-50% in 2Q26), while Birch Stream’s penetration is trending upward to

~50% (was ~40% in 2Q26). For AMD, SP5 remains mainstream while current penetration
for Venice is at <3%, and is expected to reach 3-5% by 2026 end. The company now
expects AMD Venice penetration to average 20% in 2027 (previously expect to reach
30-40% by 2027 end) while Intel’s Oak Stream is expected to reach an average
penetration of 10-15% in 2H27 (previously expect to reach 20-30% by 2027 end).
Investment view
We remain relatively cautious about Lotes’ near term business outlook, and we expect

limited room for meaningful margin improvement in the near term, mainly due to (1)
CPU socket shipments remain heavily focused toward legacy products, as the ramp up of
next gen CPU platforms (including AMD Venice and Intel Oak Stream CPU) continues to
face delays, and we believe the delays are partly due to potential shortages of key
components across the general server supply chain (including ABF substrate, memory,
PCB/CCL, etc), (2) growing contribution from margin-dilutive products starting from

2H26 (including QD and SOCAMM, which accounted for 4-5% of total 3Q26 revenue per
GSe), and (3) rising material cost could weigh on profitability as the company has limited
pricing power to fully pass on cost increases without risking market share.
However, we remain constructive on the outlook for general servers (we now expect
general server volume to increase by 20%/12%/8% YoY in 2026E-28E, was 14%/6%/6%
previously, see here), which we believe Lotes is the key beneficiary with its solid market

share in the server CPU socket market. We expect Lotes’ server revenue to see 38%
2026-28E CAGR (tracking above the 28% CAGR seen during the previous high-growth
server cycle in 2019-22), and we continue to believe server is the key margin expansion
driver given its above corporate average margin (general server socket GM at ~60% vs.
3Q26 total GM of 45%) and >55% revenue contribution throughout 2026E-28E.
Overall, we believe Lotes’ current share price has largely priced in the positive outlook

for general servers (the stock is trading at 16x+ 2027E P/E based on GSe/BBG
consensus, which is +2 STDV above the average P/E during the previous high growth
server cycle in 2019-22). The revenue contribution from higher margin new products
(including next gen CPU sockets, GPU sockets, and NPO products) is likely to remain
limited in the near-term.
We maintain our Neutral rating and lower our 12m TP to NT$1,640 (from NT1,670).
3Q26 overview
Lotes’ 3Q26 core business (OPI) was 23%/15% lower than GSe and BBG consensus, with

GM 1.6ppt/1.0ppt lower than GSe/BBG consensus, which was driven by higher material
costs and higher contribution from new AI-related products with lower margins. The
OPEX to sales ratio was 2.7ppt/2.4ppt higher than GSe and BBG consensus, due to
increasing R&D spending and labor costs. For earnings, Lotes’ NI was 18%/15% lower

than GSe/BBG consensus, mainly driven by the lower than expected GM.

Earnings revisions: 
We revise down our 2026E revenue by 5% to factor in 3Q26 actual results and revise 
down our 2027E revenue by 1% to factor in delay in new CPU socket products, but revise 
up our 2028E/2029E revenue estimates by 2%/2% to factor in the 
better-than-expected contribution from the server business. We revise down our 
2026/27/28/29E GM estimates by 1.2/2.2/1.2/0.9ppt to factor in the ongoing rising 
material costs and delay in new CPU socket products. Overall, we revise down our 
2026/27/28E earnings estimates by 11/8/1%. 
Valuation:  
Maintain Neutral rating and revise down our 12-m TP to NT$1,640 (from NT$1,670), 
based on an unchanged P/E of 16.0x (which is at the company’s average valuation since 
2020) and applied to 2027E EPS (rolled over from 4Q26-3Q27E), and factor in our 
earnings revisions.

### Page 5

Lotes is a connector supplier mainly focusing on the niche CPU socket market with more 
than 40% share in 2024, but we see pressure for its market share outlook in the coming 
years considering the intensifying competition in the server CPU market. We maintain 
our positive view on the company’s GM expansion potential longer term, which we 
believe will be mainly driven by better product mix (Venice server CPU to be released in 
2H26 and Oak Stream server CPU to be released in 2027, both with better ASP and 
margin). Meanwhile, Lotes’ CPU socket product line’s ASP/GM are higher than the 
company average, and we see scope for both to grow even faster in the new product 
generation given the larger socket size and pin count required. However, the company’s 
new product line, Quick Disconnects (UQD or MQD) for liquid cooling systems, is likely to 
deliver very limited revenue/gross profit contribution in the coming years (0.6/1.2% 
revenue and 0.4/0.8% GP contribution in 2026E/2027E) given thermal solution 
providers typically ship cold plates as integrated modules and leading thermal suppliers 
tend to secure QD supply in-house or through affiliated suppliers. We are Neutral rated 
on Lotes considering the share loss risk in server sockets and the shares trading at the 
historical peak P/E level, making valuation less attractive, despite the company’s solid 
revenue/GM expansion trend driven by socket upgrading demand. Key risks: (1) 
stronger-/weaker-than-expected server shipment growth which would raise/lower 
server CPU socket demand; (2) lower-/higher-than-expected component costs without 
product pricing adjustment; (3) slower-/faster-than-expected AI server-related 
contribution.

Valuation methodology: Our 12-month target price of NT$1,640 is based on a target 
P/E of 16.0x (in line with the company’s average valuation since 2020) applied to our 
2027E EPS.  
Key upside/downside risks: (1) Stronger-/weaker-than-expected server CPU demand, 
(2) lower-/higher-than-expected component costs, and (3) 
slower-/faster-than-expected AI server-related contribution.

Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

Co-Tech Development Corp.  | Asia Pacific
Key Japanese Competitor
Announced Price Hikes
What's new? Our supply chain checks suggest that one of Co-Tech's key Japanese
competitors notified customers earlier this week of price increases for its
electrodeposited (ED) copper foil products. The company will raise prices by 30%
for ¼ oz (9µm) and ⅓ oz (12µm) ED copper foils and by 15% for other thicknesses,
effective for shipments from December 2026 onward. This aligns with our previous

supply chain check findings published in our Nov 4 report (link).
Our view: The increases extend beyond high-end HVLP products to copper foils
used in consumer electronics. We believe this reflects rising production costs and,
more importantly, tightening supply, with positive implications for Co-Tech. We
expect Co-Tech to follow with further price increases, although likely at smaller
magnitudes given the ~10% hike it implemented in October.
Morgan Stanley Taiwan Limited+

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

### Page 1

Summary: Acter held an investor conference on 20 November, after its 3Q26 results 
release on 9 November 2026.

We have a Buy (1) rating on the stock with a 12-month TP of TWD1,415, based on a target 
PER of 20x, applied to our 1-year-forward EPS. For more information on this company, 
please refer to our initiation report, Initiation: acting on the demand for cleanrooms, on 7 
October 2026.

What’s the impact 
• 3Q26 results: margin recovered faster than we expected. Acter’s gross margin came 
in at 24.3% (improved from 20.6% in 2Q26 and 17.8% in 3Q25), above our estimate 
(23.0%) and the consensus (24.1%), thanks to the revenue recognition of large-scale 
cases with costs partially recognised in 2Q26. Thus, the operating margin of 18.9% also 
beat our estimate (17.9%) but was slightly lower than the consensus (19.1%).

• Intact global demand for cleanroom technology, the edge AI market, and rapid 
cleanroom expansion in Asia further support Acter’s order backlog. Its backlog 
reached TWD84bn at the end of 3Q26 vs. TWD46bn in 2025 and TWD30bn in 2022 
(15.3% 2022-25 CAGR), which largely outpaced the global cleanroom technology 
market’s forecast CAGR. The company sees strong clean room demand in light of 
demand from the OSAT, ODM and memory segments but the labour shortage should be 
the bottle neck for the sector. In the near and medium term (up to 2 years), the company 
expects the gross margin to be 22-28%, subject to inflation. However, Acter foresees a 
favourable gross margin trend in the long run (ie. 3-5 years) on rising oversees revenue 
contribution. There should be some upside potential for gross margin from rush orders.

What we recommend 
We reaffirm our Buy (1) rating with a 12-month target price of TWD1,415, based on a 
target PER of 20x, applied to our 1-year forward EPS. Based on our 2026/27 EPS 
estimates, the stock is currently trading at PERs of 11.6x/8.4x, vs. its past-3-year trading 
range of 7-23x (average: 12x). Key downside risks: weaker-than-expected expansion in 
the semiconductor industry; project delays or cancellations.

In the interests of timeliness, this document has not been edited.

研 究 報 告                
旺矽(6223.TT) 
產業：電子–半導體 
探針卡需求強勁挹注短期營運動能強勁、CPO 設備進度如期  
 投資評價與建議 
目前探針卡廠商評價普遍修正至20~35倍，尤其以短期營運相對穩健之廠商
(如精測、旺矽)仍相對有撐，而我們觀察公司探針卡需求強勁支撐短期營運動
能強勁，並重申CPO設備驗證進度如期、客戶數持續增加，故仍看好評價仍有
望回到合理區間之中上緣。 
 重點評論分析 
4Q26營收有望雙位數季增：公司預估4Q26營收有望季增5~10%、毛利率
維持近3Q26之高檔水準，觀察探針卡BB Ratio均維持高檔水準，且擴產
計畫不變。基於公司10月營收達22.3億元創下新高、財測⼀向較為保守，
預估營收有望季增12.7%至66.6億元，毛利率61.8%，稅後EPS 21.82元。 
積極擴增MEMS產能、重申CPO客戶需求與進度如期：展望2027年：
(1)VPC和MEMS明年底有望擴至250萬針、600萬針，擴充幅度仍具有上
修空間，當前VPC主要客戶未有升級至MEMS規劃，至於MEMS主要貢獻
則來自於美系客戶伺服器CPU；(2)insertion 2客戶將於12月通知驗證結
果，1H27有望獲得量產機訂單，至於insertion 3已如期出貨工程機給3間
客戶，並預期1H27將陸續獲得量產機訂單，惟公司預估CPO設備營收顯
著貢獻時間點將落在2028年，故我們同步調整營收、獲利。 
 投資風險 
CPO進展不如預期、探針卡擴產針數下修。

| 營運概況 
3Q26獲利優於預期：3Q26營收季增12.8%至59億元，連續七季創下歷史新高，毛利率61.7%，稅後EPS 19.18元，
其中營收、獲利均優於預期，推估主因為探針卡產品組合改善，加上於2Q26進行之探針卡報價調整完整貢獻3Q
營收(包含CPC漲價20%、取消VPC和MEMS 10%價格優惠)，另公司營業費用管控得宜也同步挹注獲利。 
估4Q26營收有望雙位數季增：公司預估4Q26營收有望季增5~10%、毛利率有望維持近3Q26之高檔水準，我們
推估動能包含：(1)維持VPC、MEMS月產能今年底分別擴至200萬針、350萬針之進展；(2)訂單出貨則觀察探針卡
BB Ratio均維持高檔水準，其中CPC今年前10個月維持滿載、目前BB Ratio接近1，至於MEMS、VPC則超過1，
考量公司探針卡交期已明顯延⻑至4~6個月，隱含本季探針卡營收仍有望持續提升。基於公司10月營收達22.3億
元創下新高、財測⼀向較為保守，預估本季營收有望季增12.7%至66.6億元，毛利率61.8%，稅後EPS 21.82元。 
積極擴增MEMS產能、重申CPO客戶需求與進度如期：展望2027年，我們觀察：(1)探針卡中，明年CPC月產能維
持50萬針、VPC和MEMS明年底則有望擴至250萬針、600萬針，產能擴充幅度具有上修空間，且強調2028年維持
積極擴產策略，訂單貢獻中，VPC主要客戶近年未有升級至MEMS探針卡之規劃，預期該客戶AI ASIC訂單將支撐
明年VPC需求，至於MEMS明年主要貢獻則來自於美系客戶伺服器CPU，且公司持續爭取潛在客戶AI ASIC訂單，
另規劃於2H27量產之PCB廠有望挹注探針卡成本優化；(2)CPO設備中，insertion 2如我們先前所提，於3Q26底
～4Q26初送樣至客戶端進行驗證，公司預估客戶將於12月通知驗證結果，根據目前客戶回饋正向，預期1H27有
望獲得量產機訂單，此外近期有另⼀客戶與公司接洽中(公司預估1H27獲得驗證結果)，至於insertion 3則已如期
出貨工程機給3間客戶，也將陸續交機給另3間客戶，預計1H27將陸續獲得insertion 3量產機訂單，惟考量接單至
出貨、裝機驗收之時間較⻑，公司認為CPO相關設備顯著營收貢獻時間點將落在2028年，我們則同步下修明年相
關設備之營收認列數量。綜上所述，公司預估明年營收有望年增至少50%、毛利率維持在60%以上；我們則預估
明年營收有望年增58.6%至344.6億元，較先前下修主要反映部分CPO設備遞延至2028年認列營收，毛利率63.2%，
稅後EPS 117.77元。

Document 196: 6515穎崴 亞東

Source PDF: all report pdf/6515穎崴 亞東.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-19
Pages: 7
Status: ok

### Page 1

◼ 投資建議: 
美系 AI GPU 與客製化 ASIC 持續開案，帶動高階測試座需求。模型預估，穎崴的 NVIDIA 
系統級測試座（SLT）營收將由 2026 年的 33.26 億元增至 2027 年的 83.45 億元，貢獻公
司逾半營收增量。公司目標於 2027 上半年將自製彈簧針月產能提升至 1,400 萬針，有助降
低外購成本，支撐全年毛利率由 40.92% 升至 42.97%。穎崴的矽光子與 CPO 測試方案已涵蓋
晶圓、封裝及模組，若客戶專案進入量產，可望帶來額外營收。 
根據模型，穎崴(6515)目標價 5,711 元。依模型預估的 2027 年 EPS 163.18 元 × 基準本益比 
35 倍估算，較 2026/11/18 收盤價 3,560 元具 60.4% 潛在上漲空間。建議買進 
◼ 未來觀察： 
1. 探針自製率&產能狀況 
2. HPC/AI 業務成長性 
◼ 公司是否涉及經營權爭奪之情事：無。 
◼ 產業概況： 
1. 半導體測試介面市場:系統測試需求高速成長。 
AI GPU、伺服器 CPU 與雲端業者自研 ASIC 擴張，帶動高階晶片封裝後最終測試（FT）與
系統級測試（SLT）需求成長。FT 把關量產電性品質；SLT 模擬實際系統與軟體運作，補足 
FT 對系統整合、長時間與極端負載之測試不足，隨晶片複雜度提升，滲透率持續走高。 
市場預估全球高階 HPC FT/SLT Socket TAM 將自 2026E 9 億美元躍升至 2028E 37 億美元，
CAGR 約 104%（SLT 達約 110%）。動能來自時間與耗材乘數效應：2025–2028E AI 晶片 
FT/SLT 測試時間 CAGR 達 15–20%（如 Rubin SLT 增至 120–150+ 分鐘），壓低單槽產出
並迫使客戶擴增平行測試槽位。隨大封裝、萬級以上高 Pin 數、高頻高速及破千瓦功耗成為
主流，高階測試座須整合訊號完整性、大電流與溫控散熱，帶動 FT Socket 單顆價值量提升 2 
至 3 倍；長時高功耗壓接亦加速探針磨損與更換，創造 4 至 5 倍耗材數量乘數效應。

### Page 2

◼ 公司簡介： 
1. 穎崴主要專注於半導體測試介面之研發設計與製造銷售，目前作為全球最大測試座
（Test Socket）供應商。主要產品涵蓋後端 FT Test、SLT Test、與 Burn-in Test 用之同軸
測試座（Coaxial Socket）、 HyperSocket、Burn-in Socket。今年與 TPI 簽訂五年合作契約
積極擴展前端晶圓測試 CP Test 用之 MEMS Probe Card，並整合主動溫控與液冷技術開
發高工耗測試逤需要的散熱測試解決方案。穎崴目前主要生產基地分布於高雄，分別為
高雄探針廠、高雄仁武廠，仁武廠包含一廠與二廠，其中仁武二廠已於 2Q27 投產。新
竹廠負責探針卡製造。 
2. 1H26 營收結構為 Coaxial Socket 48%、Probe Card 27%、PoP & Plastic Socket 14%、
Contact Element 6%、Others 3%、Burn-in Socket 2%。若按應用別區分，HPC & AI 占比
已達 70%居應用類別首位，其次 Smart Phone 8%、而 Networking／車用及其他合計 
22%。至 3Q26，依 Citi 調研，隨探針卡專案出貨階段性收尾及高階測試座放量，FT 
Test、SLT Test & Burn-in Socket 合計比重回升至 72%。其中 Coaxial Socket 預估 60%、
RF & Plastic Socket 大概 11%、Burn-in Socket 則佔 1%。至於 Probe Card 預估降至 20% 
3. 公司整體合併毛利率於 1Q26 為 43.0%、2Q26 因 MEMS 探針卡占比拉升及新產能折舊影
響降至 38.3%，3Q26 隨測試座比重提高毛利率已回升至 40.5%。各產品線方面，依摩
根大通調研，Socket 與 Contact Elements 毛利率穩定維持在 40%+。而 MEMS Probe Card 
因須外購關鍵探針元件，因此即便 2026 年生效之二期協議已改善獲利，毛利率仍僅約 
30–35%。目前穎崴 Pogo pin 自製程度高達 60%，隨自製彈簧針 pogo pin 月產能由 1H26
的 600 萬 pins 擴至 1H27 的 1,400 萬 pins，將有助進一步支撐 Socket 毛利率。 
4. 客戶比重:截至 2026 年活躍客戶超過 200 家，但營收高度集中於高階 AI、HPC、GPU、
ASIC 與伺服器 CPU 客戶。前十大客戶合計占營收比重達 90%，且 7 奈米及以下先進

### Page 3

製程產品占比高達 89%。根據摩根大通，2026 全年穎崴核心終端客戶營收占比以
Nvidia 居首，佔比大約 50–60%。其次為 AMD，預計 10–15%與 Google 約 2%。
◼ 成長動能：
1. AI GPU／ASIC 推升 SLT 與高階 FT 測試座需求)
AI 晶片朝大封裝、高 Pin 數、高功耗與高速傳輸升級，拉長測試時間並放大 SLT Test 導入
需求，使整體市場進入倍數擴張期。在 TAM 動能上，全球高階 HPC FT Test／SLT Test 測試
座市場規模預計自2026E 的 9 億美元躍升至2028E 的 37 億美元，市場預估從 2026 到 2028
相關產品的市場CAGR 將逾 100%，其中價值量更高的 SLT Test 測試座將由 7 億美元跳升至
31 億美元。隨穎崴切入 Nvidia SLT Test 供應鏈，目前市占率預估由 2026E 的 20% 升至
2028E 的30%，外加 2027 年雲端業者如 Google、AMD 的自研 ASIC 與伺服器 CPU 擴大量產，

FT Test／SLT Test 測試座占穎崴總營收比重預估將於 2027E–2028E 維持於 64%–79% 區
間。根據自有模型估算，測試座業務於 2027E 預計貢獻營收約新台幣 161 億–192 億元、
2028E 擴增至約 274 億–362 億元，整體測試座 2026–2028E 營收 CAGR 區間約為 61%–
89%。
2. HyperSocket 與液冷熱管理產品放量
下一代 AI 晶片正由約 10000 Pin 邁向20000 Pin 以上，且由於 AMD MI455、TPUv9 系列新
世代平台封裝尺寸將跨過 100×100mm 至 120×120mm 門檻，基板翹曲與大電流散熱成為測試瓶
頸。穎崴專利 HyperSocket 結合主動液冷熱管理方案可克服大尺寸封裝翹曲並維持穩定電
性，因此Pogo Pin 單針 ASP 相較既有高階同軸測試座享有約 30%溢價，帶動產品組合持續升
級，目前穎崴公司預估 HyperSocket 與液冷測試解決方案將於 2027 占總營收 5%，但考量客

戶大封裝世代切換節奏，普遍預期放量重心將落在 2028 年。依自有模型的預估，該測試解決
方案預計於2027 貢獻總營收約 3%–5%（約新台幣 9 億–15 億元），2028 有機會放大至約
5%–6%（約新台幣 21 億–32 億元），相較 2027 年呈現倍數級年增動能，並有助高階測試座
在擴產自製化下將 2027E–2028E 合併毛利率支撐於 41.1%–46.2% 區間。

### Page 4

3. 新增產能釋放與 CPO 測試
因應既有產能滿載，高雄仁武二廠已於2Q27 投產，其中新增 Socket 產能自 3 月開出並在第
二季順利認列營收，使 5,000-pin 的等效規格 Socket 月產能自 2026 下半年的 4.2k 套擴增逾
70%至 1H27 的 7.2k 套，自製 Pogo Pin 月產能亦由 900 萬 pins 增逾 50%至 1,400 萬 pins，預
估 2027 年底可達單月 1,600–1,800 萬 pins。新產能開出讓既有高階 AI 積壓的訂單順利轉
化為營收，支撐目前市場與自有模型所預估的穎崴 2027E 總營收區間達新台幣 213.5 億–
303.7 億元（YoY +45%–85%）、2028E 達 351.0 億–528.3 億元（YoY +64%–76%），對應
2026–2028E 總營收 CAGR 區間為 54%–81%（EPS CAGR 區間則達 76%–108%）。CPO 的測試
方案預計2H27 開始小量出貨，營收貢獻大約低個位數以下，至 2028 年進入量產成熟階段，

預計可以貢獻2028 年營收最高至接近 10%。
4. 1H26
1H26 合併營收 65.03 億元，HoH +61.0%、YoY +70.3%（1Q26 為 29.80 億元、2Q26 為
35.23 億元），整體毛利率為 40.5%（1Q26 為 43.0%、2Q26 為 38.3%）。稅後淨利 13.71 億
元（YoY +67.6%、HoH +60.4%；1Q26 為 6.99 億元、2Q26 為 6.72 億元），1H26 累計 EPS
達 38.24 元（1Q26 為 19.54 元、2Q26 為 18.70 元），上半年營收與獲利均創歷年同期新
高。截至最新已公告進度，累計前 9 月合併營收達 110.59 億元（年增 96.7%），同創歷年
同期新高。在產品組合與毛利率比較上，1H26 產品營收比重仍以 Coaxial Socket（48%）與
Probe Card（27%）為核心。
⚫ 與1H25（毛利率 48.9%）比較：1H26 毛利率表現明顯較差（YoY -8.4 個百分點），主

因1H25 銷售較多高毛利的 Non-MEMS Probe Card 及傳統高毛利組合，而 1H26 向
Technoprobe 外購關鍵 MEMS 探針元件的 MEMS Probe Card 營收占比擴大，上述外購元
件結構拉低 MEMS Probe Card 毛利率，使其落在 30%–35%，低於測試座及接觸元件的
40% 以上水準，對整體毛利率形成結構性稀釋。

### Page 5

⚫ 2H26E 穎崴合併營收 79.56 億元（HoH +22.3%、YoY +97.0%），毛利率 41.29%（HoH 
+0.82 ppt、YoY -0.58 ppt），歸屬母公司稅後淨利 17.71 億元（HoH +29.2%、YoY 
+107.1%），基本 EPS 48.76 元（HoH +27.5%、YoY +103.5%）。

⚫ 2026E 穎崴合併營收 144.60 億元（YoY +84.0%），毛利率 40.92%（YoY -4.34 
ppt），歸屬母公司稅後淨利 31.42 億元（YoY +87.8%），基本 EPS 87.04 元（YoY 
+85.5%）。 
5. 2026/2027 
產能與資本支出方面，仁武二廠與下一座新廠預計於 2Q27–1Q28 陸續投產，推升 2027 年探
針月產能達 1,400 萬–1,800 萬支、自製率提高至 50%–60%，5,000-pin 等效測試座月產能於 
1H27 達 7.2k 套等於穎崴整體測試座產能將在 2027 倍增，2027–2028 年度預估的資本支出介
於新台幣 20.0 億–24.0 億元，其中自有模型預估 2027 為 20.0 億到 22.0 億元之間、2028 預估
為 22.0 億到 24.0 億元之間。新產品線方面，受惠高階 Functional Burn-in 升級，2027–2028E 
Burn-in 測試座營收預估為新台幣 6.40 億–10.53 億元，2027 模型預估約 6.40 億元、2028 則
有望到 10.53 億元，占總營收約 3%成為未來關注的成長動能之一。CPO 與矽光子測試預計自 
2H27 起小量出貨，2028E 營收貢獻區間為 5%-10%不等的年營收，端看實際量產與交貨情況。。

⚫ 2027E 穎崴合併營收 238.42 億元（YoY +64.9%），毛利率 42.97%（YoY +2.05 ppt），
歸屬母公司稅後淨利 59.53 億元（YoY +89.5%），基本 EPS 163.18 元（ YoY +87.5%）。

⚫ 2028E 穎崴合併營收 407.33 億元（YoY +70.8%），毛利率 44.33%（YoY +1.36 ppt），
歸屬母公司稅後淨利 111.08 億元（YoY +86.6%），基本 EPS 304.52 元（ YoY +86.6%）。

### Page 1

HD 현대중공업 (329180/KS) 
실적&임단협 우려 해소, 발전엔진 수요 폭발적 
2026-10-29

조선/방산. 한승한, R.A 고서영 / shane.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-9992

매수(유지) 
목표주가: 640,000 원(하향) 
현재주가: 285,500 원 
상승여력: 124.2%

Signal: 3Q26, 조업일수 감소 및 환율 하락으로 시장 예상치 하회 
Key: 4Q26~1Q27 부터 본격적인 발전엔진 및 FDC 수주 모멘텀 전망 
Step: 상선 우려 감안해도 엔진&FDC 으로 밸류 매력도 충분. 매수 기회

3Q26 Review: 시장예상치 하회하는 실적 시현  
3Q26 연결 매출액 5조 7,536억원(+30.2% YoY, -9.1% QoQ), 영업이익 8,992억원
(+61.3% YoY, -13.5% QoQ)으로, 시장 예상치(9,792억원)를 하회했다. 조업일수 감소
(휴가 10 일+중대재해 4일) 및 환율 하락 영향(450 억원)과 더불어, 임단협 관련 예상 비
용 중 75%를 이번 분기 선반영 했기 때문이다. 상선 수주 빈티지는 중형 선박 기준 ‘23 년 
9.6%, ‘24년 65%, ‘25년 25%, 대형 선박 기준 ‘22년 4%, ‘23년 45%, ‘24년 31%, 
‘25년 20%로, 선가 상승효과 지속되는 중이다. 특히 해양 부문에서 루야 프로젝트의 공정
률 확대 효과 및 C/O로 180억원의 환입 발생으로 전 분기 대비 큰 폭의 이익 성장 시현했
다. 최근 키윗과 미국 내 합작 조선소 설립을 추진하며, 미 군수지원함 초도 2척 수주 및 향
후 물량 건조, 그리고 HII와의 함정 선체블록 하도급 사업 수주까지 기대할 수 있다.

투자의견 ‘매수’ 유지, 목표주가 64 만원으로 하향 
HD 현대중공업에 대한 투자의견 ‘매수’를 유지하고, 목표주가는 기존 82 만원에서 64 만원
으로 하향한다. 최근 상선 실적 피크아웃 우려에 따른 조선주 주가 급락으로 멀티플 하향 조
정했다. 하지만 상선 부문의 우려를 감안하더라도 상선 사이클과 무관한 육상 발전 엔진 및 
FDC 에 대한 글로벌 하이퍼스케일러들의 수요 확대는 지속되고 있는 중이다. 올해 공시 기
준으로 육상발전 엔진 약 1.6GW 수주한 것으로 알려졌으나, 미공시된 약 1.4GW 까지 포
함 시 총 3.0GW 이상을 수주한 상황이다. 캐파 증설 기준으로 ‘28 년 1.5GW, ‘29 년 
3GW 캐파에 해당하는 물량을 이미 대부분 채웠으며, 기존 계획 대비 빠른 준공 혹은 공정
률 확대, 더 나아가 추가적인 캐파 증설까지 고려할 수준으로 파악된다. 빠르면 올해 연말 
혹은 내년 초부터 4 행정 중속엔진&FDC 본격 수주를 통해 중장기적 신성장 동력 확보할 
전망이다. 현 주가 레벨에서 상승여력 충분히 남아있다는 판단으로, 3Q 실적 및 임단협 불
확성 해소된 현 시점에서 적극적 매수 의견 및 조선주 최선호주(Top-Pick)로 제시한다.

HD현대중공업 수정TP(원)

Document 267: AI服务器需求引爆戴尔业绩！营收翻倍、上调全年指引，1323亿美元订单积压创新高｜财报见闻

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI服务器需求引爆戴尔业绩！营收翻倍、上调全年指引，1323亿美元订单积压创新高｜财报见闻.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-24
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

戴尔 Q3 财季营收 549.37 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 103% ；⾮ GAAP 每股收益 9.45 美元，同⽐增⻓
265% ，两项指标均⼤幅超过市场预期。当季新增 AI 服务器订单 602 亿美元，期末积压订单
升⾄ 1323 亿美元。全年业绩指引三度上调，预计 2027 财年营收达 2020 亿美元，同⽐增⻓
78% ，预计 Q4 财季营收达 563 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 69% 。戴尔盘后股价波动不⼤，⽬前上涨
1.8% 。
摘要：
戴尔科技公布的最新财报显⽰， AI 基础设施投资热潮正以前所未有的速度转化为公司收⼊。第三
财季营收同⽐翻倍， AI 优化服务器收⼊增⻓近三倍，新订单和积压订单均创历史新⾼，推动公司
第三次上调本财年业绩指引。
当地时间 11 ⽉ 24 ⽇，戴尔公布截⾄ 2026 年 10 ⽉ 30 ⽇的 2027 财年第三季度业绩。财报显⽰，公司
当季营收达到 549.37 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 103% ；⾮ GAAP 每股收益为 9.45 美元，同⽐增⻓
265% ，两项指标均⼤幅超过市场预期。分析师此前平均预期营收为 493.2 亿美元、调整后每股

收益为 6.59 美元。
更重要的是，戴尔 AI 优化服务器业务继续加速增⻓。第三财季，该业务收⼊达到 220.31 亿美
元，同⽐增⻓ 291% ；公司当季新增 AI 服务器订单 602 亿美元，期末 AI 服务器积压订单达到 1323
亿美元，均创历史新⾼。
受此推动，戴尔将 2027 财年全年营收预期上调⾄ 2020 亿美元，并将 AI 优化服务器收⼊⽬标提⾼
⾄ 780 亿美元。
不过，戴尔的增⻓也呈现出明显的业务分化：基础设施业务成为业绩增⻓的核⼼引擎，⽽ PC 业
务增速明显放缓。
与此同时，内存芯⽚供应紧张、零部件成本上涨正在给服务器⼚商带来新的定价和利润率挑
战。如何在扩⼤ AI 服务器交付规模的同时维持盈利能⼒，将成为市场接下来关注的重点。
财报发布后，戴尔盘后股价波动不⼤，截⾄发稿，上涨 1.8% 。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

AI 服务器业务成为增⻓核⼼，积压订单突破千亿美元
戴尔第三财季的增⻓⼏乎由基础设施业务驱动。
财报显⽰，基础设施解决⽅案集团（ ISG ）当季营收达到 419.74 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 198% ；营业
利润达到 71.18 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 308% ，营业利润率升⾄ 17.0% ，⾼于上年同期的 12.4% 。
其中， AI 优化服务器收⼊达到 220.31 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 291% ，成为最主要的增⻓来源。传统服
务器及⽹络业务收⼊也达到 150.13 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 235% ；存储业务收⼊为 49.30 亿美元，同
⽐增⻓ 24% 。
订单情况同样强劲。戴尔当季新增 AI 服务器订单达到 602 亿美元，期末积压订单升⾄ 1323 亿美
元。公司副董事⻓兼⾸席运营官 Jeff Clarke 表⽰，戴尔正在对积压订单进⾏严格筛选，评估客⼾
资质以及资⾦、⼟地和电⼒资源的可获得性。
Clarke 还表⽰，⽬前全球已有超过 7500 家客⼾部署戴尔 AI Factory 解决⽅案。通过在发货前完成

整机柜测试，并在交付后六⼩时内完成部署，戴尔希望缩短客⼾从设备交付到模型运⾏的时
间。
这⼀业务扩张也推动戴尔进⼀步调整⾃⾝业务结构。 Clarke 在财报声明中表⽰，基础设施解决⽅
案集团如今已经贡献了公司绝⼤部分利润和增⻓。
全年业绩指引第三次上调， AI 服务器收⼊⽬标提⾼⾄ 780 亿美元
强劲的季度业绩促使戴尔再次提⾼全年业绩预期。
公司预计， 2027 财年全年营收将达到 2020 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 78% ，⾼于此前 1920 亿美元的预
测。全年⾮ GAAP 每股收益预期从 25.50 美元上调⾄ 29.68 美元，同⽐增⻓ 188% 。
AI 优化服务器业务的全年收⼊⽬标也从 740 亿美元提⾼⾄ 780 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 216% 。
对于第四财季，戴尔预计营收将达到 563 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 69% ；⾮ GAAP 每股收益预计为 8.40
美元，同⽐增⻓ 116% 。

### Page 3

这⼀展望同样⾼于华尔街此前预期。根据路透社援引的 LSEG 数据，分析师此前预计戴尔 2027 财
年全年营收约为 1944.8 亿美元，
此外，戴尔董事会批准新增 250 亿美元股票回购授权。公司第三财季通过股票回购和股息向股东
返还了 49 亿美元，前三财季经营活动现⾦流达到 107.65 亿美元。
内存成本上涨，服务器⼚商开始向客⼾转嫁压⼒
在 AI 服务器需求快速增⻓的同时，供应链成本问题正在成为⾏业新的变量。
内存芯⽚价格上涨尤其值得关注。此前，戴尔在 2027 财年第⼆季度的监管⽂件中已经披露， AI
优化解决⽅案需求强劲，加上内存制造商产能受限，导致全球供应紧张以及内存组件成本⼤幅
上涨。公司还指出，部分组件采购成本正在上升。
11 ⽉ 22 ⽇，《⾦融时报》报道称，戴尔、惠普企业、联想和超微电脑等服务器⼚商正在要求部分
客⼾为已经签订合同的 AI 服务器⽀付更⾼价格，部分订单的涨价幅度达到 25% ⾄ 45% 。
报道援引知情⼈⼠称，部分 GPU 服务器价格上涨了 20% ⾄ 30% ，内存占成本较⾼的 CPU 服务器

节点涨幅甚⾄更⼤。内存价格上涨使服务器⼚商难以继续按照此前合同价格交付设备。
戴尔还因⼀笔服务器交易⾯临法律纠纷。英国交易公司 XTX Markets 在爱尔兰起诉戴尔，争议涉
及⼀笔价值 5.49 亿美元的服务器合同。 XTX ⽅⾯指称，戴尔要求在原合同⾦额之外额外⽀付
6200 万⾄ 7000 万美元。相关纠纷仍在进⾏中。
这意味着， AI 服务器市场虽然需求旺盛，但⼚商的盈利能⼒并不只取决于订单规模，还取决于其
能否将不断上涨的内存和其他零部件成本转嫁给客⼾。
值得注意的是，戴尔此次财报并未单独披露内存涨价对第三财季利润的具体影响，也没有给出
未来内存成本的量化预测。因此，不能仅凭营收和利润增⻓，就认定公司已经完全消化了内存
成本压⼒。
PC 业务增⻓放缓，消费端需求⾯临压⼒
与基础设施业务的强劲表现相⽐，戴尔传统 PC 业务增⻓相对疲弱。
财报显⽰，客⼾端解决⽅案集团（ CSG ）第三财季营收为 128.86 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 3% ；营业利
润为 7.04 亿美元，同⽐下降 6% ，营业利润率从上年同期的 6.0% 降⾄ 5.5% 。

其中，商业 PC 业务收⼊ 113 亿美元，同⽐增⻓ 6% ；消费 PC 业务收⼊ 15.86 亿美元，同⽐下降
15% 。
这表明，企业客⼾设备更新需求仍提供⼀定⽀撑，但消费端表现相对疲弱。

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
• Unemployment and output both show a common pattern
of downturns being short, sharp and irregular, while the
subsequent recoveries and expansions are longer and
slower. The eminent economist Milton Friedman likened
this pattern to plucking on a string. The causes are unclear,

though there are some plausible candidates.
• If we take the “plucking” pattern seriously, there are clear
policy implications. Standard models used by central banks
do not allow for this pattern and therefore systematically
overstate how often capacity is tight. And when a
downturn does occur, the “plucking” view argues for
forceful stabilisation policy.
• We believe the current slow upward grind in
unemployment in Australia is a different phenomenon

and expect slow growth rather than a sharp downturn. If
people start thinking current inflation pressures demand a
1980s-style “Volcker disinflation”, though, it could just end
up plucking the economy.
Look at a graph of the unemployment rate for almost any
western economy, and you will notice a few things. The
big increases are sharp and sudden, while the subsequent
recoveries are slow and last longer. The timing of those upward
spikes is irregular, with no obvious pattern. The height of the

spike in unemployment is a good predictor of how far the rate
declines in the recovery that follows. By contrast, neither the
length of an expansion phase of declining unemployment, nor
the size of the decline predict the size of the next downturn.
This pattern is a far cry from the notion of a business
cycle, where output and unemployment oscillate round
some average or trend. There is something different about
downturns. They are not just like expansions but a bit slower.

The eminent economist Milton Friedman described this as a
“plucking” model. The economy, he suggested, was a bit like a
guitar string. Most of the time it stayed close to the fretboard.
There is a physical maximum amount of production possible
at any one time. Occasionally, though, the economy could be
plucked away from that maximum, with output falling and
unemployment rising sharply.
Remarkably, the mainstream macroeconomic literature that
central banks rely on has engaged little with this feature of the

data. Forecasting models used by the RBA and its peers do not
produce this pattern. Some authors have looked at “plucking”-
type models, though. Their analysis confirmed that downturns
predict the size of the following expansion, but the reverse
Is the economy ‘plucked’?
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
is not true. Expansions do not “die of old age” by building up
imbalances. Australia went for 30 years without a proper
downturn: it turns out that this is entirely possible provided

nothing bad enough happens.
These authors further argue that the maximum production level
during expansions is buffeted by small shocks, some temporary,
others positive and permanent – think new technologies and
ways of doing things, or a policy change that encourages
workforce participation. Downturns, by contrast, are triggered
by large shocks that are usually temporary and arrive at irregular
intervals. Some argue that it is best to think of economies as

having three phases: normal expansions, which can continue
for long periods; shorter-lived downturns; and subsequent
recovery phases where growth can outstrip normal rates and
unemployment declines, but at a slower pace than it rose.
Some downturns do not end in full recovery, however. This
observation links to another strand of research showing that
the output losses associated with financial crises and wars are
not fully recovered. The experience after the Global Financial

Crisis shows this pattern in a number of advanced economies.
Standard models would interpret this as slower growth in
potential output, perhaps feeding the Australian tradition of
hand-wringing about insufficient productivity or reform.
While the pattern in the data is clear, the causes of “plucking”
are far from settled, with some authors seeing boom–bust
dynamics as inherent in the economic system rather than
driven by shocks. Some recent papers – including preliminary

RBA work – look to certain frictions in the labour market
for an explanation. These include search frictions, which
arise because workers and firms sometimes require time to
find a good match with each other. Another at least partial
explanation is that workers resist absolute wage cuts but
accept falls in purchasing power when dollar wages are
constant in the face of positive inflation.
An objection to this line of research is the finding by some BIS

researchers that the plucking pattern is clearer in countries
with more flexible labour markets – mostly English-speaking
ones. The literature focusing on labour market frictions also
does not explain where the originating pluck comes from,
“Downturns are usually short, sharp and
arrive at irregular intervals; expansions are
longer and slower."

### Page 3

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
only why the data have the dynamics they do when those
shocks occur. Something must be causing the “pluck” shock,
if indeed it is a shock. The BIS paper suggests one possibility:
the authors find that output in the manufacturing and trade
sectors shows a clearer pluck pattern than construction.

Construction downturns tend to linger, perhaps because of
their connections to indebtedness and financial crises.
If we take seriously the patterns in the data showing
asymmetric downturns and the plucking pattern Friedman
described, there are clear policy implications. Currently, the
models underlying central bank forecasts are symmetric by
construction. They therefore systematically understate spare
capacity during expansions. They will find a positive output

gap ahead of a downturn, even when there was no evidence of
overheating at the time. This oversight risks policy mistakes.
The asymmetric nature of downturns also means that the
benefits of macroeconomic stabilisation policy are mostly
about cushioning downturns and hastening recoveries. In
normal times, the benefits will be small.
We also note that the RBA paper mentioned above hypothesised
a short-run NAIRU and a long-run NAIRU that are both pushed

around by actual unemployment in a path-dependent pattern.
This is an argument for “holding on to the employment gains”
that may have been missed by people whose mental model of
the economy is fully symmetric, without any plucking.
The plucking model started from empirical observations
about large downturns. The kind of slow grind upwards in
unemployment seen in Australia in the 2010s and in the past
couple of years does not fit this pattern. It is a qualitatively

different phenomenon requiring other explanations, including
perhaps the path-dependent drag upwards of the feasible
unemployment rate by the actual unemployment rate.
It is these kinds of considerations that underlie our current
forecast for slow growth and not a downturn, even though this
week’s labour market data were concerning. A further 25 basis
points or so of rate increases is unlikely to be the thing that
causes a severe downturn. Typically, downturns are not just

slower expansions but are qualitatively different phases that
require a trigger.
The one thing that makes me nervous is that the plucking
literature finds that every downturn is different. The famous
opening line to Tolstoy’s Anna Karenina, “Happy families are
all alike; every unhappy family is unhappy in its own way,”
would seem to apply to economies as well. And in at least one
case, a forceful tightening of monetary policy was the trigger
for a downturn. The so-called “Volcker disinflation” brought

US inflation under control in the early 1980s. That episode is
widely regarded as a success, despite the output costs. In the
current circumstances, some policymakers and commentators
might be tempted to believe that the same medicine is needed;
nobody wants inflation expectations to become entrenched,
after all. However, as RBA Board member Ian Ross has noted,
the institutions of the 1970s that produced wage–price spirals
are no longer operative. So there is no guarantee that a similar

approach nowadays would be necessary or as effective.
Watch out then, if the discourse turns to arguments that
countries “need a recession” to get inflation under control. A
Volcker-style policy could just end up plucking the economy.
Forecast changes
• We have upgraded our WPI forecast following this week's data
release, with our year-end forecast now 3.4%yr.
• We have also upgraded our near-term unemployment rate,
with a year-end forecast of 4.9%.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: risks mount
risks are skewed to the upside. FOMC members who spoke
throughout the week also kept the focus on price risks, and called
for the FOMC to act swiftly. Governor Waller again proposed a
50bp hike in December; others seemingly favour a 25bp hike at

the December and January meetings, as Westpac forecasts, and
continuing to tighten further in 2027 if necessary.
Very clearly, the labour market and activity growth are not
of concern for US policy makers. Retail sales gave further
justification for this view this week, the control group
surprising to the upside with a 0.6% gain in October after
a material upward revision to September (0.5%). That said,
housing is under considerable pressure, pending home sales

falling 17.1% over the past year, and housing starts back at
2019 levels despite a 9.9% rebound in October. Persistent
weakness in this sector will, in time, have implications for both
consumption and investment.
China’s October data round was the other key international
development of the week. Again, it provided a call for pro-
active stimulus in scale. In the month, annual retail sales
growth almost stalled at 0.4%yr and the decline in fixed
asset investment broadened outside property, with capital

expenditure now down 8.1% across the economy year-to-
date. Industrial production again highlighted that its output to
satiate global demand which is holding up aggregate growth;
though, even here, momentum softened in October, annual
growth in production slowing from 5.5%yr to 4.8%yr. It is not
only activity that remains under pressure, but also wealth,
with new / existing home prices 0.15% / 0.24% lower in the
month. As we continue to emphasise, to prove effective,

stimulus needs to affect change in both activity and wealth
and do so in a sustainable manner.
Australia’s labour market disappointed again in October,
employment falling 43.5k following September’s 36.4k
decline. On a three-month average basis, job gains over the
year have now slowed to 1.1%yr, materially below current
growth in the working-age population, 1.7%yr. As a result, the
unemployment rate rose to a new post-pandemic high of 4.9%
despite the participation rate falling another 0.2ppts to 66.6%.

While employment growth’s descent initially reflected softer
momentum in hiring, now we are seeing evidence of a material
increase in redundancies. Most affected workers look to have
remained in employment to date, but persistent job shedding
raises the risk of a faster and more persistent uptrend in the
unemployment rate, potentially above our baseline peak
forecast of 5.2% (at mid-2027) and most certainly before the
late-2028 peak of 5.1% the RBA expects.

As such, the robust 1.1% (3.5%yr) rise in the Q3 Wage Price
Index warrants close scrutiny. The Q3 increase was skewed
towards those on awards and enterprise arrangements – pay
outcomes directly tied to the Fair Work Commission’s decision
and collective bargaining. Individual arrangements meanwhile,
the most responsive to current and expected labour market
conditions, accounted for their smallest share of a Q3 wage
increase since 2019. Business survey detail also suggests labour

cost growth will normalise quickly after the award increase.
Deteriorating economic conditions are also increasingly evident
in housing. The Westpac Housing Sentiment Index fell 2.2%
to 83.2 in November, among the weakest readings since 2005.
Interestingly, there remains considerable latent demand: almost
13% of consumers intend to purchase a home over the next
year, and relatively few plan to sell. For now, though, weak
sentiment, high interest rates and expectations of further price

declines are keeping would-be buyers on the sidelines. These
factors also pose risks to the broader growth story.
In the US, abstracting from the Anthropic IPO, the outlook for
inflation remained the critical theme. The October FOMC minutes
highlighted that inflation is currently being supported by tariffs
and energy prices as well as the AI buildout, with "several
[members] judg[ing] that price pressures were increasingly
extending beyond industries directly affected by data center

construction”. Participants "assessed that medium- and longer-
term inflation expectations continued to be consistent with the
Committee's 2 percent objective", although persistent above-
target inflation is a threat to the status quo, and perceived
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist
“Australia’s labour market delivered a second
consecutive shock in October, pushing annual
employment growth well below working-age

population growth."

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
Sunny side up
This week we published our final Economic Overview
for 2026. Titled “Sunny side up”, the report shows that
New Zealand’s economic recovery is continuing. Activity
expanded by 2.6% in the year to June, and recent indicators

point to further moderate growth. Overall, GDP is forecast
to grow by 2.8% in both 2027 and 2028. But this remains a
two-speed recovery: export-facing industries and regional
centres are enjoying stronger incomes, while households
and businesses tied to the major metropolitan centres are
still contending with high inflation, a soft labour market and
a subdued housing market.
The global backdrop remains broadly supportive. On an export-
weighted basis, New Zealand’s trading partners are forecast

to grow by 3.1% in 2026, 2.9% in 2027 and 2.8% in 2028.
Stronger growth in the United States and East Asia outside
China, helped by the continuing artificial intelligence investment
buildout, is offsetting some of the drag from higher energy
prices. The central assumption underpinning these forecasts is
that a Middle East agreement eventually restores safe passage
through the Strait of Hormuz, allowing Brent crude to fall
towards US$77 a barrel in late 2027 and US$71 in late 2028.

Against that backdrop, the external sector is the clear bright
spot. Merchandise export volumes are estimated to have risen
by more than 4% this year, with dairy, meat and horticulture
joined by stronger output of manufactured metals, machinery
and equipment. Favourable growing conditions, high world
prices and a low New Zealand dollar have driven this growth.
Services exports are performing even more strongly and
are on track to grow by around 9% this year. Foreign visitor

arrivals continue to recover strongly, with Australians and
Chinese accounting for about two-thirds of the increase
seen this year. In 12-month rolling terms, visitor numbers
are expected to soon move above their 2019 peak, while
other services exports will likely continue their longer-run
outperformance.
Agriculture is expected to remain a major source of strength.
Farmgate returns are expected to stay elevated despite high
energy and shipping costs and an expected modest appreciation

in the New Zealand dollar. Meat export prices should remain
high in real terms over the next couple of years, with beef
supported by firm US demand. Kiwifruit and apple prices are
also expected to remain strong, while log prices should be
broadly stable to slightly firmer in 2027. Dairy prices – at least
powders – appear to have received an additional lift from El
Niño-related drought risk and strong global demand for protein.
We have raised our forecast milk price for the current season

by 50 cents to $10.25 per kilogram of milk solids – still ahead
of Fonterra’s own forecast, which was revised up to a $10.10
midpoint this week. We have also lifted our 2027/28 season
forecast to $9.75, reflecting a more gradual expected rise in the
New Zealand dollar than foreseen previously.
At this point, the strength offshore has not yet translated
into an equally convincing recovery for households. Average
household earnings grew only 2.9% in the year to June, while

high inflation pushed real wage growth into negative territory.
Real per capita household spending was flat over the year, and
weak house prices have limited growth in household wealth.
Spending should improve gradually during 2027 as export
income circulates through the economy, employment recovers
and inflation eases. Even so, the strongest gains are likely to
remain in regions directly exposed to agriculture and tourism.
The labour market has been unusually slow to respond to

improving GDP. Unemployment has climbed to an 11-year high
of 5.7%, and employment growth has failed to keep pace with
population growth. The pattern is best described as “low-
fire, low-hire”: firms avoided the scale of layoffs that might
normally accompany a downturn but may now have enough
staff to accommodate the early stages of a recovery without
immediately stepping up recruitment. Employment appears
to have begun turning higher and hiring intentions have

recovered, but unemployment is unlikely to decline materially
until next year. We expect it to ease to 5.1% by the end of
2027 and 4.5% in 2028.
Housing remains a restraint. National house prices have fallen
about 1.7% this year, sales have weakened and properties are
taking longer to sell. Adjusted for inflation, house prices are on
course for a fourth consecutive annual decline, and we expect
them to fall by around 33% between the 2021 peak and the

end of 2027. That adjustment is improving affordability and
supporting first-home buyers, but it also encourages existing
owners to save rather than spend. As with other economic
indicators, regional differences remain pronounced, with areas
benefiting from agricultural and tourism income outperforming
the major centres.
Residential construction should nevertheless rise during
2027, supported by a pipeline of more than 40,000 consented
homes, much of it medium-density development in Auckland

and Canterbury. The durability of that upswing is uncertain,
however, because financing costs, building costs and weak
house prices remain significant obstacles. Commercial
construction is closer to finding a floor, although office and
retail development is likely to stay subdued for a while given
elevated vacancy rates.
Darren Gibbs
Senior Economist

### Page 6

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
6
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Business investment has been more resilient. Capital equipment
spending rose strongly in the June quarter, and imports point to
further gains despite higher freight costs and a weaker currency.
Confidence rebounded after the initial shock from the Middle

East conflict and has held up despite renewed oil price pressure.
Investment will remain uneven, but cash-rich agricultural
businesses are well placed, and computing equipment and
software are contributing to growth.
Inflation is the main complication. Consumer prices rose 3.7%
in the year to September and annual inflation is expected to
reach 4.3% in December. Higher global oil prices have played
an important role, but the problem is broader than fuel. Core

inflation measures remain around 3%, operating costs are
elevated and firms’ pricing intentions have strengthened.
Headline inflation should fall as energy prices ease and could
temporarily dip below 2% in 2028, but underlying inflation is
expected to decline much more slowly.
That persistence means the RBNZ will likely need to tighten
policy by more than it currently forecasts. The OCR is expected
to reach 3% by year-end, but we think the neutral rate may

be closer to 3.75%, in contrast to the RBNZ’s 3% to 3.5%
estimate. As growth strengthens and core inflation proves
sticky, we expect the OCR to peak at 4% in September 2027
and remain there for about a year. Medium- and long-term
interest rates are therefore likely to settle above the unusually
low levels seen in the decade before the pandemic.
Finally, fiscal conditions should improve as stronger nominal
activity and above-target inflation lift tax revenue. However,

spending pressures and higher debt-servicing costs will be
difficult to avoid. We expect the operating balance before
gains and losses, excluding ACC (OBEGALx), to return to
surplus in 2028/29, while net core Crown debt peaks near
43.7% of GDP in 2027/28. The unresolved election adds
uncertainty, however; the forecasts assume no major near-
term change in policy direction.
Turning to this week’s key economic news, RBNZ Governor
Anna Breman and fellow MPC member Prasanna Gai spoke

to audiences in New Zealand and Australia respectively. We
thought that Breman’s comments were reasonably balanced.
On the hawkish side, Breman noted that some indicators of
economic activity had been stronger than expected. Perhaps
of greater note, she also said that this month’s partial October
CPI data (discussed below) indicated that indirect impacts
from the fuel price shock might be growing following a more
muted than expected impact in the September quarter. On

the dovish side, she noted subdued domestic demand, which
she said was making it difficult for firms to pass on higher
costs. She also referred to weak labour market data (including
weak wage growth) and the stability seen in medium-term
inflation expectations (also discussed below). Following the
October review Breman had commented that the chances of a
December rate hike were “high”.
In our view, Gai’s commentary leaned more on the dovish
side. He reiterated his concerns about the implications for NZ

of a bursting of the AI bubble and described recent inflation
and inflation expectations data as “quiescent”. In response to
a question, he indicated that he considered that news since
the October meeting had validated his decision to vote for a
pause at that meeting. The general tone of Gai’s commentary
suggested it would not be a surprise if he voted to extend the
pause at next month’s meeting. Even if he were to vote for
a hike, it seems likely that Gai would regard this as simply a

tentative step towards the roughly 3.25% neutral rate that the
Bank projected in the September Monetary Policy Statement.
On the data front, the partial CPI data for October pointed
to weaker than expected food price inflation but firmer than
expected increases in travel costs. The latter was probably
responsible for Breman’s comments above, but given the
month-to-month volatility of this data we would be hesitant
to read too much into one month’s outcome. These opposing

impacts netted out and so the outcome was consistent
with our forecast for a lift in annual inflation to 4.3% in the
December quarter. Meanwhile, the RBNZ’s long-running
survey of professional forecasters reported a lift in year-ahead
inflation expectations. However, encouragingly, the closely
watched 2-year-ahead measure was little changed at 2.36%.
Moreover, 5-year-ahead and 10-year-ahead expectations –
which had been singled out for special attention by RBNZ

Chief Economist Conway in a recent speech – fell to 2.17%
and 2.13% respectively. In both cases, these were the
lowest readings in almost two years. Finally, encouragingly,
the BusinessNZ services PMI continued its gradual trend
improvement in October, rising to a 3-year high of 51.7.

### Page 7

We expect this positive momentum to have carried into Q3 
2026, with further solid gains in residential building, private 
non-residential building and private engineering construction, 
particularly electricity-related projects. Public infrastructure 
investment will offset some of this strength, with the drag that 
began last year continuing as major projects reach completion.
AUS: October CPI (%mth)
November 25, Last: 0.8%, Westpac f/c: 0.4%  
Mkt f/c: 0.4%, Range : 0.3 to 0.6
We expect the October CPI to rise 0.41%mth with the annual 
pace lifting from 4.3%yr to 4.8%yr, the fastest pace in the 
short history. Westpac expects this to be the peak in current 
acceleration in annual inflation. Our seasonally adjusted CPI 
estimate lifts 0.7%mth, a small step up from the 0.6% increase 
in September, highighting the seasonal softness in the month. 
Transport makes the largest contribution (+0.35ppts), almost 
entirely due to auto fuel, with smaller contributions from 
clothing & footwear (+0.06ppts), household goods & services 
(+0.05ppts) and meals out & takeaway (+0.04ppts). The 
main offsets are recreation & culture (–0.18ppts) with fruit & 
vegetables, utilities and others providing marginal offsets.
For more details please see “October CPI Preview“.  
AUS: October Trimmed Mean (%mth)
November 25, Last: 0.2%, Westpac f/c: 0.4% 
Mkt f/c: 0.4%, Range : 0.3 to 0.5
Our October Trimmed Mean estimate is 0.42%mth, a step up from 
0.3% in September, taking the annual pace to 3.7%yr from 3.6%yr 
and lifting the six month annualised pace to 4.2%yr, from 4.0%yr, 
which is also the fastest pace in the short history of the series. 
Rents and dwellings continue to underpin the TM estimates. 
We see risks as slightly tilted to the upside. Our estimates 
allow for a greater degree of price resetting in areas such as 
meals out & takeaway and personal and household services, 
but this adjustment proves broader than our assumptions. 
Fuel levy surcharges are another source of upside risk, 
including increases by Woolworths and Australia Post. The 
key downside risk remains housing, particularly new dwelling 
prices, which surprised to the downside in recent months.

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.8
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
NZ: Q3 retail spending (%qtr)
Nov 23, Last: -0.5, Westpac: 1.4
The June quarter saw a larger than expected 0.5% fall in
retail spending, with high fuel prices squeezing households’
spending power. However, even with the drag from high
fuel prices, core spending (excl. fuels and vehicles) remained

resilient, rising by 0.7%. With fuel prices dropping through
much of the September quarter, we expect overall spending
levels will lift by 1.4%. Importantly, with lower fuel prices
putting money back into households’ pockets, that’s likely to
be underpinned by firm gains in core spending categories.
AUS: Q3 Private CAPEX (%qtr)
Nov 26, Last: –3.6%, WBC f/c: 10.0%
Mkt f/c: 4.5%, Range: 2.0% to 10.0%
Private capex declined 3.6%qtr in Q2 2026 as data centre-

related machinery and equipment investment pulled back
sharply. With nominal ADP equipment imports surging
83%qtr to another record high, we expect a sizeable rebound
in the Q3 figures. Buildings and structures should also find
support as the broader strength in private non-residential
approvals through the first half of 2026 works through the
pipeline. Overall, we expect private capex to grow a strong
10.0%qtr in Q3, with data centres again leading the charge.

Est 3 for 2026/27 capex plans, after our adjustment, is
consistent with impressive nominal growth of 17.9%yr and
real growth of 15.3%yr. We expect Est 4 unadjusted plans for
2026/27 to come in at $221bn, a 15.5% increase on a year ago,
indicative of the scale of investment yet to occur.

### Page 1

A New Keynesian ‘plucking’ model of unemployment and inflation
PRELIMINARY AND INCOMPLETE
Anthony Brassil* and Callum Ryan*
Research Discussion Paper
20XX-YY
December 2025
*Economic Research Department
Reserve Bank of Australia
The views expressed in this paper are those of the authors and should not be attributed to the
Reserve Bank of Australia. Any errors are the sole responsibility of the authors.
Author: ryanca at domain rba.gov.au
External Communications: rbainfo@rba.gov.au

https://doi.org/10.47688/rdp20XX-YY

### Page 2

Abstract
We construct a New Keynesian model with search-and-matching frictions in the labour market that
can generate the nonlinear ‘plucking’ dynamics of unemployment in the data: unemployment tends to
spike upwards and then slowly decline. The model features endogenous layoffs, a ‘slippery’ job ladder,
downward nominal wage rigidity, and convex recruiting costs. It generates both the ‘deepness’ and
‘steepness’ asymmetries in unemployment dynamics, and broadly matches the response of labour-

market flows in a downturn. The model endogeneously generates a Phillips curve relationship that
is strongly nonlinear and state-dependent, and a short-run non-accelerating rate of unemployment
(NAIRU) that fluctuates asymmetrically over the business cycle. We also show that cyclical properties
(e.g. shock volatility, nominal rigidities, policy regime) can have a significant effect on the long-run
NAIRU via agents’ precautionary response to future uncertainty.

1. Introduction
The unemployment rate in the data has strongly nonlinear dynamic (Figure 1). It spikes sharply in
occasional downturns, then declines slowly but persistently in recoveries.1 It is right-skewed in both
levels (i.e. asymmetric ‘deepness’) and in changes (i.e. asymmetric ‘steepness’) (Sichel 1993). Gross
flows between unemployment and employment display similar nonlinearities. Standard search-and-

matching models of the labour market struggle to generate these nonlinearities in unemployment
and labour-market flows (Ferraro 2023).
If this asymmetric pattern stems from frictions within the labour market, what implication do those
frictions have for the relationship between inflation and unemployment? Research using nonlinear
structural models to explore the asymmetric dynamics in the labour market tend to abstract from price
frictions and inflation dynamics (e.g. Dupraz, Nakamura and Steinsson 2025; Ferraro 2018; Petrosky-

Nadeau, Zhang and Kuehn 2018), while in the New Keynesian literature, the usual approach of
linearising equilibrium conditions excludes these nonlinear dynamics by assumption. The 2020s
inflation surge has reignited research into the nonlinearity of the Phillips curve, and the extent to which
this nonlinearity stems from frictions within the labour market (e.g. Benigno and Eggertsson 2023).
But this literature tends not to model the labour market in as much detail.

In this paper, we explore the extent to which a detailed search-and-matching model of the labour
market with pricing frictions can match the nonlinear dynamics in the labour market. We then use
the model to examine the implications of these nonlinearities for the Phillips curve relationship and
the non-accelerating rate of unemployment (NAIRU).
1 Friedman (1964) uses the ‘plucking’ description for the asymmetric way in which output seems to be occasionally ‘plucked’

below trend, before recovering back to trend.

### Page 5

2
We start with a general-equilibrium search-and-matching model similar to Bloesch, Lee and
Weber (2024), in which firms post wages and vacancies and set prices (subject to nominal rigidities
and recruiting costs), and workers search (while unemployed and on the job) and decide whether to
accept and quit jobs. Policy steers aggregate demand to balance inflation and unemployment targets.
We extend this model to include endogenous layoffs, downward nominal wage rigidity, and a ‘slippery’

job ladder (drawing on Dupraz et al (2025)).2 We find that these features - together with convex
recruiting costs - are collectively key to achieving both the asymmetric spikes in unemployment when
shocks occur and the slow speed in the subsequent recovery. The model is sufficiently detailed to
broadly match the dynamics of unemployment and gross flows, but tractable enough that we can
obtain the global nonlinear solution with rational expectations and aggregate uncertainty. This is

important because agents’ precautionary response to future uncertainty is a significant determinant
of outcomes, especially the long-run NAIRU.
We show that the model can generate substantial asymmetry in both ‘deepness’ and ‘steepness’
(comparable to or greater than in the data). The response of unemployment, job-finding, and
separations after a stylised negative demand shock broadly matches the response of these variables in
downturns over the past 50 years. Aggregate demand shocks have an especially asymmetric effect,

because the effect on inflation amplifies the effect of the downward nominal wage rigidity. When
supply shocks occur, the inflation response somewhat eases the downward nominal wage constraint.
The model endogenously generates a Phillips curve relationship that is strongly nonlinear and state-
dependent. Conditional on some initial state of the economy, the Phillips curve is much steeper for
low rates of unemployment. When the initial unemployment rate is higher, the Phillips curve shifts to

the right, and when the initial level of real wages is higher, it shifts upwards.
We can also use the model to simulate a ‘short-run NAIRU’, which is the unemployment rate consistent
with eliminating inflationary pressure from the labour market at some cyclical horizon (e.g. 1-2 years).
This short-run NAIRU varies asymmetrically with the state of the economy. It tends to rise when
unemployment is high, but also when unemployment has fallen rapidly (because real wages increase).

Finally, we can use the ‘stochastic steady state’ of the model to explore the determinants of the long-
run NAIRU, i.e. the unemployment rate consistent with stable inflation in the absence of shocks (but
where agents still expect that shocks may occur in the future). We find that when firms account for
the interaction between aggregate risk and asymmetric wage adjustment costs in their wage-setting
decisions, it generates a trade-off between unemployment variance and steady-state unemployment.

For example, if policy places greater weight on reducing the gap between unemployment and the
long-run NAIRU (instead of the gap between inflation and target), it tends to increase the long-run
NAIRU. This is because by mitigating fluctuations in labour demand, policy reduces the risk of hitting
downward nominal wage rigidities. Firms are then more willing to set higher real wages in steady
state, which reduces the optimal amount of employment in steady state.

The remainder of this introduction discusses the literature. Section 2 outlines the model. Section 3
evaluates the extent to which it can match the nonlinear dynamics in labour-market data. Section 4
considers the implications for the Phillips curve and NAIRU.
2 By ‘slippery’ job ladder, we mean that workers recently hired out of unemployed are much more likely to be laid off.

### Page 6

3
Literature review. The cyclical asymmetries in unemployment are established empirically by e.g.
Sichel (1993), McKay and Reis (2008), and Dupraz et al (2025). Ferraro (2023) analyses the ability of
the baseline Diamond-Mortensen-Pissarides (DMP) model to produce these asymmetries, and finds
that it cannot generate the ‘steepness’ asymmetry (i.e. right-skewness in unemployment changes).
Petrosky-Nadeau et al (2018) finds that the DMP model can generate the ‘deepness’ asymmetry given

large enough shocks.
Dupraz et al (2025) and Ferraro (2018) propose search-and-matching models that can generate
sharp spikes and slow declines in unemployment. We take several features from Dupraz et al (2025),
including downward nominal wage rigidity and a ‘slippery’ job ladder. Pizzinelli, Theodoridis and
Zanetti (2020) explore the state-dependence of unemployment fluctuations in a model with
endogenous layoffs and on-the-job search.3 Adjemian, Karamé and Langot (2021) estimate a

nonlinear model with endogenous separations, downward wage rigidity, worker heterogeneity and
segmented markets, and show that it can generate similar asymmetries in unemployment, hiring,
and separations to the data.
Our definition of a ‘short-run NAIRU’ is similar to the concept discussed by Hall and Kudlyak (n.d.), who
argue that the natural rate of unemployment may vary cyclically and track the actual unemployment
rate during recoveries. Hall and Kudlyak (2022) discusses the ability of DMP-style models to generate

the slow persistent recovery in unemployment that occurs after recessions. Fujita and Ramey (2007)
show that adding vacancy adjustment costs to a DMP model can produce a more sluggish response
for vacancies and hiring after a downturn, slowing down the recovery in unemployment. Mercan,
Schoefer and Sedláček (2024) suggest a congestion mechanism, whereby new hires and incumbent
workers are imperfect substitutes in production, which also propagates unemployment after a shock.

This literature above generally abstracts from price rigidities and inflation dynamics. There is a
substantial literature incorporating labour-market frictions into New Keynesian models (see e.g.
Blanchard and Galí 2010). Recent examples include Moscarini and Postel-Vinay (2023), Faccini and
Melosi (2025), and Bloesch et al (2024). These papers focus on the role of on-the-job search and wage
competition between firms in inflation dynamics. We adopt a similar model for wage setting, drawing

on the tractable formulation in Bloesch et al (2024). But these papers use linear approximations.
The literature on downward nominal wage rigidity explores the extent to which it generates a
nonlinear Phillips curve and asymmetric cyclical dynamics (see e.g. Kim and Ruge-Murcia 2009;
Abbritti and Fahr 2013; Daly and Hobijn 2014; Benigno and Antonio Ricci 2011; Schmitt-Grohé
and Uribe 2022; Benigno and Eggertsson 2023; Benigno and Eggertsson 2024). Lepetit (2020)

also constructs a New Keynesian model with asymmetric unemployment dynamics, although the
asymmetry stems from the convex relationship between the job-finding rate and unemployment.
This literature does not model the labour market in as much detail (e.g. endogenous layoffs, on-the-
job search, job ladder) and does not seek to match the ‘plucking’ property in unemployment to the
same extent.
3 Fujita and Ramey (2012) also analyse versions of the DMP model with endogenous separations and on-the-job search,

but their focus is not on asymmetries in unemployment rate dynamics

### Page 7

4
2. Model
The model consists of firms, workers, and a policy rule. We start by outlining the firms’ problem
and optimal choices. Firms’ optimal decision-making drives most of the model’s mechanics. We then
discuss workers and the policy regime, which are modelled more simply. 5 contains a full list of
equilibrium conditions, as well as details of the calibration and solution method.
2.1 Firms
Firms make decisions around hiring, separations, and price- and wage-setting.

Hiring: Firms choose how many vacancies to post, V i t . These vacancies generate Mi t = qtV i t matches,
where qt is determined by a matching function and depends on aggregate matching efficiency and
labour market tightness. A proportion 1 − ϕ E t of these matches are unemployed searchers (where ϕ E t
is the share of unemployed searchers in total searchers, weighted by search intensity).
Firms must screen matches. Then the individual productivities of employed matches are revealed.

Firms make offers to those with productivities above some threshold xi t , which is 1−F i t of them (where
F i t = F(xi t ) and F is the CDF of the individual productivity distribution). For unemployed matches,
firms have no information about their individual productivity and make offers to all of them. This
feature proxies for the idea that firms may be more uncertain about the capabilities of unemployed
applicants.
Matches with offers choose whether to accept them; Ace t (W i t ) and employed matches and Acu t (W i t )

of unemployed matches do so, where W i t is the real wage paid by the firm. These job-acceptance
probabilities are characterised in the description of workers below.
The number of new hires is given by
Hi t = ϕ E t Mi t (1 − F i t )Ace t (W i t ) + (1 − ϕ E t )Mi t Acu t (W i t ).
Define Hi,u t = (1 − ϕ E t )Mi t Acu t (W i t ) as the number of hires from unemployment.
Recruiting costs include a per-vacancy cost and a per-match ‘screening’ cost:

Separations: There are two types of separation: quits and layoffs. Each period, Qt (W i t ) of firms’
existing employees quit (either to unemployment or to another job). Just as firms’ hiring rate is

increasing in the real wage they offer (via the job-acceptance probabilities Ace t (W i t ) and Acu t (W i t )),
their quits rate is decreasing in the real wage by the firm. This function is characterised in the section
below on workers.
Workers’ probability of being laid off depends on whether they are insecurely or securely attached.
Insecurely attached employees are all those who have just been hired out of unemployed in
the previous period. Before aggregate shocks are revealed, some portion of new hires from

unemployment, δ u, (i.e. who were hired the previous period), draw a new productivity from

productivity distribution). The layoff probability for securely attached employees will be lower than
for insecurely attached employees both because δ < δ u and the distribution F has less mass at low
values than G.
